<result># GPU / Accelerator Backends — Exploration Report

All paths relative to `C:\Users\thaku\Downloads\InNova`. Line refs are `file:line`.

## 1. Backend abstraction, dynamic loading, probe/fallback order

**Abstraction.** `include/quant/backend.h:7-31` defines a `BackendType` enum with **24 values** (CPU_SCALAR … CPU_ZENDNN); abstract `ComputeBackend` (backend.h:63+). Concrete adapters live in `src/backend.cpp` (1568 lines): CPU_SCALAR (:50), CPU_AVX2 (:89), CPU_AVX512 (:178), IGPU_SHARED (:228), GPU_DIRECTX (:285), GPU_CUDA (:369), GPU_METAL (:435), GPU_SYCL (:499), GPU_CANN (:552), RPC (:603), RAM_SWAP (:666), DISTRIBUTED (:702), NPU_OPENVINO (:757), GPU_VIRTGPU (:808), GPU_WEBGPU (:859), CPU_ZENDNN (:910). Factory at `backend.cpp:961-991`; unknown type falls to `CPUScalarBackend` (:989).

**Dynamic-loading-without-SDK.** No vendor SDK headers anywhere — every backend `LoadLibraryA`/`dlopen`s the runtime and resolves raw `PFN_*` typedefs:
- CUDA: `nvcuda.dll` / `libcuda.so[.1]` (`src/gpu_compute_cuda.cpp:715-723`) — driver API only.
- Vulkan: `vulkan-1.dll` / `libvulkan.so.1` via `vkGetInstanceProcAddr` (`src/gpu_compute_vulkan.cpp:255-268`).
- DirectX12: real `d3d12.h`/HLSL compiled at runtime via D3DCompile (`src/gpu_compute.cpp:7-30`, `src/gpu_compute_full.cpp:405-409`) — Windows-only.
- Metal: dlopen of ObjC + Metal.framework with embedded MSL source string (`src/gpu_compute_metal.cpp:167-168`, MSL at :6).
- Others: SYCL PI symbols from `sycl7.dll`/`libsycl.so.7` (`gpu_compute_sycl.cpp:206-212`), Ascend `libascendcl.so` (:57-61 of cann), HIP `amdhip64.dll`+hiprtc (`gpu_compute_hip.cpp:283-287`), OpenCL `OpenCL.dll` (:55-57), MUSA `musa_runtime.dll` (:37-39), Hexagon NN `libhexagon_nn_skel` (:36-38), zDNN `zdnn.dll` (:33-37), OpenVINO-C `openvino_c.dll` (:18-20), WebGPU `webgpu.dll/libwebgpu.so` (:18-20), ZenDNN resolves `zendnn_sgemm` (`gpu_compute_zendnn.cpp:60`). RPC is raw Winsock/POSIX sockets (`gpu_compute_rpc.cpp:1-12`).

**Probe &amp; selection.** `probe_hardware()` fills a `HardwareProfile` via per-backend `is_*_available()` probes (`backend.cpp:1301-1367`); each probe try-inits the singleton backend in a try/catch (:1117-1177). `select_optimal_backend()` priority (`backend.cpp:1374-1471`): **CUDA &gt; Metal &gt; Vulkan &gt; DX12 &gt; SYCL &gt; CANN &gt; RPC &gt; OpenVINO &gt; VirtGPU &gt; WebGPU &gt; ZenDNN &gt; CPU_AVX2 (if model fits 50% free RAM) &gt; IGPU_SHARED &gt; RAM_SWAP &gt; CPU_SCALAR**.

**Gaps/stubs in selection itself:**
- `is_hip_available/is_hexagon_available/is_zdnn_available/is_musa_available/is_opencl_available` hard-return `false` (`backend.cpp:1141-1145`) — those backends can never be auto-selected despite having implementations.
- `is_rpc_available()` always `true` (:1137-1139) so RPC would win over CPU on any machine where all GPU probes fail.
- **Bug:** selection can return `BackendType::GPU_VULKAN` (:1388-1392) but `ComputeBackend::create` has **no GPU_VULKAN case** (:961-991) → falls through to scalar CPU; the `VulkanBackend` class is only reachable via `gpu::get_vulkan_backend()`.
- Every adapter op falls back to `math::*` when `!is_initialized()` (e.g., CUDA gemm :384-386, SYCL :514, CANN :567).

## 2. Vulkan backend + iGPU zero-copy

`src/gpu_compute_vulkan.cpp` (1349 lines): full instance/device/queue setup without Vulkan headers semantics hacks — physical device picked preferring discrete GPU by parsing `VkPhysicalDeviceProperties` out of a raw byte buffer (`deviceType` read from word offset 7, :309-321), queue families parsed as raw 48-byte records (:331-346). Five **SPIR-V compute shaders embedded as uint32 arrays**: relu (:84), gelu (:108), silu (:128), add (:140), mul (:155). Pipeline path: descriptor layout with 1 storage-buffer binding + 32-byte push constants → shader module → compute pipeline, cached in an unordered_map keyed by SPIR-V bytes (:470-506); each dispatch allocates descriptor set, binds pipeline, pushes `[n]`, dispatches gx=(n+255)/256, submits with fence and waits (:508-578). Memory: `allocate()` creates DEVICE_LOCAL storage buffers returned as `VkBuffer*` handles (:758-771); upload/download via staging copies (:786-801). **Only the 5 elementwise ops run on GPU**; gemm/gemv/softmax/rms_norm/layer_norm/moe_gather/scatter/flash_attention are straight CPU fallbacks (`:986-1068`, incl. comment that GEMV previously wrongly dispatched the relu shader, :994-995). `is_initialized()` returns true even in CPU-fallback mode (:755). The `vk_*` tensor wrappers (:1145+) silently return zeros if the backend didn&apos;t init (e.g., `vk_flash_attention` :1145-1156); `vk_cross_entropy[_grad]` are pure CPU loops regardless (:1159-1200).

**Zero-copy trainer** `src/igpu_zero_copy.cpp`: same loading pattern; allocator probes for **unified memory = HOST_VISIBLE|HOST_COHERENT|DEVICE_LOCAL in one type** (`has_unified_memory()` :601-612). `allocate_unified()` requests exactly that triple, falls back to HOST_VISIBLE|HOST_COHERENT (:685-690), persistently maps and returns the host pointer (:703-715); frees tracked by mapped-pointer lookup (:718-731). `available_unified()` sums DEVICE_LOCAL heaps (:753-760). However `upload_direct/download_direct` are plain `memcpy` either way (:733-751), and `IGPUZeroCopyTrainer` computes with **scalar CPU kernels over the mapped memory**: `forward_linear` runs `cpu_zc_gemm` on unified buffers (:836-861), attention/RMSNorm/SwiGLU/cross-entropy are scalar loops (:884-955), `train_step` just computes loss (:957-960). So zero-copy allocation is real; GPU-side math is not wired up.

## 3. CUDA backend

`src/gpu_compute_cuda.cpp` (1065 lines): loads driver DLL only (:715-723), resolves ~20 symbols including `cuModuleLoadData`, `cuLaunchKernel`, async memcpy, streams, `cuMemHostRegister` (:728-749). Ships **hand-written PTX (.version 6.5, .target sm_30) as one embedded string** (:51-668) with **17 kernels**: gemm (naive 16x16-tile, one FMA per thread per k, :56-98), relu (:100), silu (:111), gelu as fast-gelu x·sigmoid(1.702x) via ex2.approx (:122), add (:133), mul (:145), scale (:157), fill (:168), softmax row kernel (:177), rmsnorm (:207), rope (:233), attention (full QK^T softmax PV per query block, non-causal, :284-405), reduce_sum/max with shared-memory tree (:407/:467), moe_gather/moe_scatter (:~510s/:566), layernorm (:605). Init: cuInit→cuDeviceGet→cuCtxCreate→two streams (compute+copy, :751-756)→`cuModuleLoadData(PTX)` (:758)→17 `cuModuleGetFunction` (:760-776). Host page-locking + async upload/download exposed in header (`include/quant/gpu_compute_cuda.h:47-54`) and implemented via `cuMemHostRegister`/stream launches (~:1000-1065). The `GPUCUDABackend` wrapper in backend.cpp only routes **gemm** through the GPU with per-call alloc/upload/compute/download/free (:383-404); every other op goes to `math::*` (:405-421) — so most CUDA kernels are implemented but not exercised through the ComputeBackend interface.

## 4. CPU SIMD kernel library

- **math.cpp** = portable scalar baseline, compiled only `#if !QUANT_AVX2` (`src/math.cpp:662`); FP16 conversions :517-553; **FP8 E4M3/E5M2 at math.cpp:564-646** — bit-exact decode (E4M3 exp==15 → NaN :564-572; E5M2 inf/NaN :574-582), round-to-nearest encode with overflow saturation (:584-622), vector converters (:624-631), and `fp8_gemm` which quantizes A/B elementwise then accumulates in f32 (:633-646).
- **math_avx2.cpp** (compiled only under QUANT_AVX2, :9-11): GEMM is a **6x16 register-blocked kernel** with `_mm256_set1_ps` broadcasts and dual `_mm256_fmadd_ps` accumulators (:19-75); AVX2 gemv/dot/axpy/squared-norm (:96-190). The FP8 family is re-implemented here (:660-668) — still scalar inner logic, but `fp8_gemm` batch-quantizes whole matrices into uint8/f32 buffers before the triple loop.
- **math_avx2_tiled.cpp** + **math_tiled.h**: cache-blocked GEMM — TILE_M=N=K=64 &quot;tuned for L1/L2&quot; (`math_tiled.h:13-17`); A/B panels packed to `alignas(64)` buffers (:274-288, :341-342), next-panel prefetch hint (:384-386), four transpose variants with 6x16 micro-kernels (:117-240), fused bias/ReLU/GELU/SiLU epilogues (:432-484). Header API `gemm_tiled[_bias/_act/_bias_act]` (`math_tiled.h:25-93`).
- **math_avx2_tensor.cpp**: tensor-shaped wrappers over the same AVX2 primitives (guarded QUANT_AVX2 at :9).
- **AVX512 status**: `math_avx512.cpp` compiles only `#if defined(__AVX512F__)` (:8) and provides polynomial exp approx (:22+) plus elementwise/norm/gemv only per `math_avx512.h` — **no AVX-512 GEMM kernel**. The `CPU_AVX512` backend writes its own naive `_mm512_fmadd_ps` outer-product GEMM inline in `backend.cpp:183-201` and delegates everything else to an internal CPUAVX2Backend member (:179, :203-215). CMake adds math_avx512.cpp conditionally (`CMakeLists.txt:62-63`).
- **kernel.h/kernel_production.h**: `kernel.h` = codebook/LUT GEMM family (TL1/TL2 LUT, QUANT8/QUANT4 codebook, Q3/Q6/Q12/Q24) plus `scalar/tiled/avx2[_bt]_gemm` (`include/quant/kernel.h:11-81`), implemented across `src/kernel_tl.cpp, kernel_quant8.cpp, kernel_quant4.cpp, kernel_q3/q6/q12/q24.cpp, kernel_gemm.cpp`. `kernel_production.h` = ISA-dispatched (AVX2&gt;SSE4.1&gt;scalar, runtime-detected) tiled/batch GEMV for the Q0/QUANT4/QUANT8 packed-weight formats (`include/quant/kernel_production.h:1-40`), impl in `src/kernel_production.cpp`.

## 5. Tests vs untestable scaffolding

**Have tests** (registered in `tests/CMakeLists.txt`; listed in suite at :209-211):
- `tests/test_math.cpp` — ReLU/SiLU/RMSNorm correctness only; **no FP8 or tiled-GEMM tests**.
- `tests/test_kernel.cpp` — scalar_gemm/tiled_gemm/avx2_gemm checked against exact 128.0 results (lines 14-45).
- `tests/test_gpu.cpp` — smoke test: probe_hardware + auto_select_backend + `is_cuda_available()` with graceful-fallback messaging (:21-31).
- `tests/test_cuda_backend.cpp` — init/alloc/free on real hardware; body skipped entirely when no NVIDIA driver (:15-27 prints &quot;Graceful Fallback OK&quot;).

**Untested / scaffolding-only:**
- No tests at all for: Vulkan backend, igpu_zero_copy, gpu_compute_full (DX12), Metal, SYCL, CANN, HIP, OpenVINO, VirtGPU, WebGPU, ZenDNN, OpenCL, MUSA, Hexagon, zDNN, RPC, math_tiled/math_avx512 functions, kernel_production, or any FP8 path.
- Scaffolding evidence: WebGPU and VirtGPU `gemm` are plain scalar triple-loops reporting a fake fixed 1 GB of memory (`gpu_compute_webgpu.cpp:70-92`, `gpu_compute_virtgpu.cpp:50-71`); their init merely dlopens a library or opens `/dev/dri/renderD128` (`virtgpu :29-31`) without ever dispatching work. MUSA/Hexagon/zDNN/OpenCL/HIP init fails cleanly when the runtime lib is absent (`gpu_compute_musa.cpp:42`, `hexagon:41,49`, `zdnn:41,52`, `opencl:60-87`, `hip:290-306`) but are additionally locked out of auto-selection by hardcoded `false` (`backend.cpp:1141-1145`). The RPC backend always reports available (`backend.cpp:656`, :1137-1139) with per-op CPU fallback inside each call (:618-650). The zero-copy trainer&apos;s GPU claim is cosmetic: uploads are `memcpy` (`igpu_zero_copy.cpp:733-751`) and all math is scalar (:765-818).

**Net assessment:** genuinely functional paths are CPU (scalar/AVX2/tiled + codebook kernels), DX12 on Windows (runtime HLSL), CUDA gemm-on-GPU when a driver exists, and Vulkan&apos;s five elementwise shaders; everything else is well-structured probe-and-degrade scaffolding whose value is graceful fallback rather than acceleration.</result>

<summary>Agent Explore task &quot;Emit missing inference stack report now&quot; completed.</summary>
<result># InNova Model + Inference Stack — Audit Report

## 1. Transformer architecture (`include/quant/transformer.h`, `src/transformer.cpp`)

- **Block**: pre-norm LLaMA-style `TransformerBlock`: RMSNorm → Attention → residual → RMSNorm → SwiGLU FFN; optional GPT-NeoX parallel residual (`use_parallel_residual`, transformer.cpp:493-512). Activation supports SiLU/SwiGLU/GeGLU/GELU/ReLU (transformer.cpp:428-481).
- **GQA**: real config support (`num_kv_heads &lt; num_heads`) but implemented as *materialized memcpy expansion* of K/V to all heads before the kernel (transformer.cpp:300-320) — correct but wastes memory/bandwidth; no grouped-GEMM path.
- **RoPE**: half-dim rotate-neox style, AVX2-vectorized apply (transformer.cpp:186-228). Scaling modes all present in one cache builder: Linear (position/factor), NTK (θ·α^(d/(d−2)) base change), YARN (smoothed frequency blend on beta_fast/beta_slow boundaries) — transformer.cpp:155-176.
- **Confirmed audit finding**: `yarn_attn_factor` is an unnamed discarded parameter (transformer.cpp:138) and `mscale`/`inter_len` are `(void)`-discarded (transformer.cpp:176). The YARN mscale attention-surface correction is never applied; long-context outputs will be under-scaled.
- **Attention dispatch** (`Attention::forward`, transformer.cpp:251-404): autograd path uses engine ops; inference path: B&gt;1 bypasses KVCache and does full-window causal attention from pos 0 (transformer.cpp:281-286); B==1 appends to cache, ropes at `cache.context_len()`. Kernel choice: `S_full &gt; 64` → FlashAttention, else naive O(S²) loops.
- **FlashAttention** (`src/flash_attention.cpp`): genuine FlashAttention-2-style tiled online softmax (row max/sum rescaling, block=32 for D≤64 sized to L1, AVX2 FMA dots, fully-masked-block NaN guard at :114-122, in-score dropout). Real, single-threaded CPU only.
- Dead internal classes defined but never used anywhere else (grep confirms references only within transformer.cpp): `KimiDeltaAttention` (:515-666), `AttentionResidual` (:668-710), `FP8_E4M3/E5M2` + `fp8_matmul` scalar triple-loop (:713-791), duplicate `MultiHeadLatentAttention` (:793-869), duplicate `MultiTokenPredictionHead` (:871-892).

## 2. Model/config system (`include/quant/model.h`, `src/model.cpp`)

- `DenseModel` = Embedding → N×TransformerBlock → final RMSNorm → lm_head, plus MTP heads (`mtp_num_heads` extra Linear(hidden→vocab), model.cpp:25-28).
- `.quant` format (&quot;QUA1&quot; v1): header carries raw `sizeof(TransformerConfig)` blob (model.cpp:208-209, 336-341) — config is a raw struct memcpy, fragile across versions/ABI. Tensor table maps names → blocks of 32K-element Q32 (raw FP32) or codebook-quantized blocks (Q8/Q4/Q2 via k-means-ish `CodebookQUANTx::train`, model.cpp:216-307).
- Naming: HF-style `tok_embeddings.weight`, `layers.N.attention.{q,k,v,o}_proj.weight[/bias]`, `layers.N.ffn.{gate,up,down}_proj.weight`, `norm.weight`, `lm_head.weight` (model.cpp:132-155).
- `load()` refuses non-`.quant` files with a marketing message (&quot;~40-45% more compute-efficient... contact owner&quot;, model.cpp:326-333). Load silently skips missing/mismatched tensors (`assign` no-ops on numel mismatch, model.cpp:352-357) — a truncated file loads without error.
- **Bug**: MTP head weights are neither saved nor loaded — absent from `collect_named_tensors` (:132-155) and from the load assign list (:359-374); after reload, mtp_heads are random init while `mtp_forward` (:395-420) still emits their logits.
- `Model::save()` base writes header-only files (no weights, model.cpp:383-392); `HybridMoeModel::load/save` are no-op stubs `(void)p;` (hybrid_moe_model.h) — hybrid MoE models cannot be persisted.

## 3. KV cache design (`src/kv_cache.cpp`, `kv_cache_quant4.h/cpp`)

- Plain `KVCache`: per-layer contiguous `{1,H,max_seq,d}` K/V tensors, mutex per cache, append/get_range/get_all, `resize`. Works with the dense Attention path.
- &quot;FP8&quot; quantization is mislabeled: `quantize_fp8_block` (:15-34) is symmetric per-64-element **int8 absmax quantization** stored as uint8, not E4M3. Dequant is int8*scale per scalar (slow loop, :184-203).
- Paged caches are genuinely implemented: `PagedKVCacheBase` with 2-level (4M tokens) / 3-level (1T token claim) radix-like page tables of 4096-entry tables, per-block LRU counters, dirty tracking, disk spill/reload via raw binary files in TEMP (kv_cache.cpp:426-532), memory-budget eviction loops (:649-656, :804-809). BUT grep shows `PagedKVCache*` is used only by `tests/paged_kv_4m_test.cpp` — no model, generator, or server path ever attaches it. It&apos;s real but **orphaned infrastructure**.
- `kv_cache_quant4.h` claims &quot;~170× compression&quot;; stores packed 4-bit indices + FP16 codebooks per block — implementation exists (kv_cache_quant4.cpp), again not wired into inference.
- MLA latent caching claimed in headers (&quot;The latent is cached&quot;) is false: `MLAAttention::forward` discards positions/mask/cache and calls `forward_naive` full recompute every step (mla_attention.h:47-51); same for `KDAAttention` (kda_attention.h:46-50). No incremental decode exists for either.

## 4. Inference optimization reality (`src/inference_opt.cpp`, `speculative_decoder.cpp`)

- **Speculative decoding v1 (`SpeculativeDecoder`) is broken**, confirmed:
  - `generate()` verifies drafts against the target using `p_draft = 1/vocab` uniform instead of actual draft probabilities (inference_opt.cpp:240) — acceptance math `min(1, p_target·vocab)` nearly always accepts, defeating rejection sampling guarantees; residual distribution correction (:248-264) is likewise built on the wrong p_draft.
  - `verify_tokens()` feeds each draft token alone as `{1,1}` input with position i but no context/cache (:146-150), so draft logits are context-free garbage.
  - Draft tokens are pushed into `output` before verification (:196) then rolled back by resize on first reject (:247) — works, but the target&apos;s bonus token after full acceptance is never added, so accepted gamma rounds produce no net speedup gain beyond draft length.
  - Null-model fallbacks fill logits with `v/vocab` ramps and uniform randoms (:198-200, :222-226) — placeholder scaffolding.
- `SpeculativeDecoderV2` + `RejectionStats` exist in the header (:246-301) with tree attention claims; impl reuses the same flawed pattern family.
- Separate `speculative_decoder.cpp` (SOPS-integrated) uses exact-match acceptance only (`draft_token == target_token`, speculative_decoder.cpp:103) — greedy-only, simpler, correct for argmax but not distribution-preserving sampling.
- **Continuous batching is fake**: `ContinuousBatching::step` builds a padded mask (`build_attention_mask`) then throws it away — calls `model_-&gt;forward(batch_input, batch_pos)` with **no mask and no KV caches** (:331-336); `kv_caches_` vector is allocated and never used. Greedy argmax only; batch forward through a cache-less dense model re-prefills everything each step. Not usable continuous batching.
- `CompressedKVCache` comment says &quot;QUANT4 ... 2-bit per element&quot; (:395-397) but actually ternarizes values (-1/0/+1 at fixed ±0.1 threshold) into byte arrays — zero compression vs float storage layout claims.
- `FP8Inference` (D9) routes through the scalar `fp8_matmul` in transformer.cpp:765-791 — quantize/dequantize per element inside an O(MNK) loop; correctness demo, ~1000× slower than FP32.
- Also present: PrefixCache (per-prefix KVCache clones), TreeDecoder (raw-pointer tree), INT8Inference, DynamicBatcher, RequestScheduler (priority/deadline heap), InferenceMemoryPool, EmbeddingEndpoint/Reranker, GrammarDecoder — mostly thin scaffolding; tests exist (tests/test_inference_opt.cpp, test_inference_batch.cpp).

## 5. Production/serving layer

- Two independent HTTP servers:
  - `ModelHTTPServer` (production_core.cpp:227-767): hand-rolled socket server, select()-based accept loop + worker thread pool, per-request timeout, Content-Length body assembly, CORS OPTIONS, routes `/v1/completions` + `/completions` (:619+), streaming via chunked `send_stream_response` (:444). Plus `Logger`, JSON `AppConfig` parser (:805-1250).
  - `HTTPServer` (src/http_server.cpp, header http_server.h): token-bucket RateLimiter, thread pool, metrics counters, routes `/health`, `/v1/models`, `/v1/completions`, `/v1/chat/completions`, `/v1/embeddings` (:600-660) driven by injectable callbacks (`set_generate_callback`, load/unload/embeddings callbacks).
- `production_api.cpp` is a plugin loader (dlopen/LoadLibrary + create_plugin factories), not route registration.
- Thread model: blocking sockets, thread-per-connection pool, no async I/O; generation holds the worker thread; no scheduler integration — continuous batching/speculative paths are not connected to serving.
- Sampler/generator stack is real and modest: `Sampler` top-k/top-p/temp/repetition-penalty over raw logits (sampler.h); `Generator` and `StreamingGenerator` with stop strings, logprobs, atomic stop flag (generator.h). Single sequence, single KVCache each.

## 6. Tokenizers

- `BPETokenizer` (bpe_tokenizer_bpe.cpp): **real BPE training** — iterative pair-count + merge loop (:64-90), save/load of vocab+merges (:120-155). Byte-fallback vocab of 256 singles at init. Plus `UnigramTokenizer`, `WordPieceTokenizer`, `ByteLevelBPETokenizer`, `MultiLingualTokenizer`, `SentencePieceTrainer` wrappers (bpe_tokenizer.h:43-201) with train+load; advanced/unicode variants in bpe_tokenizer_advanced.cpp / bpe_tokenizer_unicode.cpp.
- `Qwen35Tokenizer` (qwen35_tokenizer.h/cpp): **merge-load only** — reads HF `tokenizer.json` (+byte-level byte map) via `load_from_dir` (qwen35_tokenizer.cpp:147-150); no training.
- `K3Tokenizer` (k3_tokenizer.h): wraps `BPETokenizer`, vocab_size 152064, Kimi special ids (im_start 151644/im_end 151645), chat template helper; `load_from_dir`; tests/test_k3_tokenizer.cpp exists.

## 7. Backend selection (`src/backend.cpp`)

- `ComputeBackend::create` factory dispatches all 23 `BackendType`s (:975 region); availability probes per API. `probe_hardware()` (:1301+) collects CPUID/OS/RAM/VRAM flags. `select_optimal_backend` (:1369-1466) is a simple priority cascade: CUDA &gt; Metal &gt; Vulkan &gt; DirectX &gt; SYCL &gt; CANN &gt; RPC &gt; OpenVINO &gt; virtgpu &gt; WebGPU &gt; ZenDNN &gt; AVX2-CPU (if model ≤ 50% free RAM) &gt; iGPU-shared &gt; RAM_SWAP &gt; scalar. `benchmark_operation` for micro-GFLOPS. Note many exotic backends (MUSA, Hexagon, zDNN, WebGPU...) have thin stub impls (e.g., gpu_compute_hexagon.cpp 1.8K, gpu_compute_zdnn.cpp 1.9K) — enumerated far beyond what&apos;s production-real.

# InNova — Full Project Map

## Kya hai ye
Pure **C++20, zero-dependency AI engine** with apna `.quant` mixed-precision format. Koi PyTorch/GGUF nahi — tensor se lekar training, fine-tuning, inference, HTTP serving tak sab khud ka code. Scale: **141 src files (~125K lines), 26 libs + 14 tools + 12 benches + 56 tests ≈ 111 build targets**, 130 headers. Format family v3: **105 formats** (base Q1–Q32 + K L/M/H tiers + GRP variants + half-BPW + MXQ mixes).

## Live mission (workbench.md)
`master_plan_v2_20260822` — Phase 0–1 done (bench fixes, bug hunt), **Phase 2 anti-fake audit in progress**. Recent wins: Q8_GRP ab GGUF Q8_0 ko dono datasets pe haraata hai (+0.42/+0.40 dB), speed round 1 ne encode 2–8x fast kiya quality loss ke bina. Owed: fresh full green ctest run. Known failing: `test_quant_mix` (QUAD_MIX@3.5_GRP budget 128>120) + `test_fuzz_codec` → Phase 3 L036.

## ✅ Jo SACH mein solid hai
- **Codec encode**: true-MSE golden search + LS-seeded scale fitting (`block_codec.cpp:298-350`), signed affine mins, closed-form q1 scale — genuinely sophisticated
- **Bench harness**: median-of-7 + warmup, gaussian AND real-trained weights, faithful GGUF baselines in-file
- **Dense training**: autograd (12 ops, checkpointing), Adafactor genuinely factorized + 13 optimizers, EWC/replay continual learning, LoRA-style RankAdapterEngine, **GRPO real+tested**
- **Kernels**: FlashAttention-2 style tiled online softmax, AVX2 6×16 register-blocked GEMM, cache-blocked tiled GEMM, bit-exact FP8 E4M3/E5M2
- **GPU**: DX12 runtime-HLSL (Windows), CUDA driver-API + embedded PTX 17 kernels, Vulkan 5 elementwise shaders
- **Tokenizers**: real BPE training loop; Qwen35/K3 merge-load

## ⚠️ Jo toota/nakli hai (claim_ledger C-01..C-25 confirmed)
- **BPW contract violation (sabse bada)**: GRP formats wire pe +0.5 BPW likhte hain par types.h/registry base BPW claim karta hai; K_GRP budgets overflow karte hain; fuzz test sirf enum 0..37 check karta hai — violators (38–104) kabhi audit hi nahi hote
- **Real bugs**: affine grid mismatch bits==1/6 (encode thirds, decode fifteenths); codebook serialize/deserialize magic mismatch → roundtrip hamesha throws; MXQ_GRP enc ceil(c/32) vs dec ceil(c/64)
- **MoE**: experts+routers ko **ZERO gradient** milta hai (dispatcher autograd chain todta hai); aux loss dummy `{1,1}` constant ≡1.0; prefetcher kabhi construct nahi hota (`init_prefetcher` header-only)
- **RL**: PPO/DPO bina backward ke shells; RLHF comparisons fabricated (chosen==rejected)
- **Loss-scale bug**: mixed precision enable karne pe updates ~128x shrink ho jaate hain (grads seed 1.0, unscale phir bhi divide karta hai)
- **Inference**: spec-decode v1 broken (p_draft=1/vocab, context-free verify); continuous batching fake (mask banata hai, phenk deta hai, KV caches unused); MLA/KDA cache discard karte hain; YARN mscale discarded; paged-KV 4M orphaned (sirf test use karta hai); MTP heads train/infer/save — kuch mein nahi
- **Multimodal/AGI**: encode_image → `{30000}` constant; T2I proxy; AGI flywheel ka sandbox/test/rollback harness real hai par solution generation = 32 hardcoded templates; self_eval "MMLU" keyword-matching toy
- **Backend selection bugs**: GPU_VULKAN select hota hai par factory mein case hi nahi → silently scalar CPU; HIP/MUSA/etc hardcoded false; RPC hamesha "available"

## 1. Backend abstraction, dynamic loading, probe/fallback order

**Abstraction.** `include/quant/backend.h:7-31` defines a `BackendType` enum with **24 values** (CPU_SCALAR … CPU_ZENDNN); abstract `ComputeBackend` (backend.h:63+). Concrete adapters live in `src/backend.cpp` (1568 lines): CPU_SCALAR (:50), CPU_AVX2 (:89), CPU_AVX512 (:178), IGPU_SHARED (:228), GPU_DIRECTX (:285), GPU_CUDA (:369), GPU_METAL (:435), GPU_SYCL (:499), GPU_CANN (:552), RPC (:603), RAM_SWAP (:666), DISTRIBUTED (:702), NPU_OPENVINO (:757), GPU_VIRTGPU (:808), GPU_WEBGPU (:859), CPU_ZENDNN (:910). Factory at `backend.cpp:961-991`; unknown type falls to `CPUScalarBackend` (:989).

**Dynamic-loading-without-SDK.** No vendor SDK headers anywhere — every backend `LoadLibraryA`/`dlopen`s the runtime and resolves raw `PFN_*` typedefs:
- CUDA: `nvcuda.dll` / `libcuda.so[.1]` (`src/gpu_compute_cuda.cpp:715-723`) — driver API only.
- Vulkan: `vulkan-1.dll` / `libvulkan.so.1` via `vkGetInstanceProcAddr` (`src/gpu_compute_vulkan.cpp:255-268`).
- DirectX12: real `d3d12.h`/HLSL compiled at runtime via D3DCompile (`src/gpu_compute.cpp:7-30`, `src/gpu_compute_full.cpp:405-409`) — Windows-only.
- Metal: dlopen of ObjC + Metal.framework with embedded MSL source string (`src/gpu_compute_metal.cpp:167-168`, MSL at :6).
- Others: SYCL PI symbols from `sycl7.dll`/`libsycl.so.7` (`gpu_compute_sycl.cpp:206-212`), Ascend `libascendcl.so` (:57-61 of cann), HIP `amdhip64.dll`+hiprtc (`gpu_compute_hip.cpp:283-287`), OpenCL `OpenCL.dll` (:55-57), MUSA `musa_runtime.dll` (:37-39), Hexagon NN `libhexagon_nn_skel` (:36-38), zDNN `zdnn.dll` (:33-37), OpenVINO-C `openvino_c.dll` (:18-20), WebGPU `webgpu.dll/libwebgpu.so` (:18-20), ZenDNN resolves `zendnn_sgemm` (`gpu_compute_zendnn.cpp:60`). RPC is raw Winsock/POSIX sockets (`gpu_compute_rpc.cpp:1-12`).

**Probe & selection.** `probe_hardware()` fills a `HardwareProfile` via per-backend `is_*_available()` probes (`backend.cpp:1301-1367`); each probe try-inits the singleton backend in a try/catch (:1117-1177). `select_optimal_backend()` priority (`backend.cpp:1374-1471`): **CUDA > Metal > Vulkan > DX12 > SYCL > CANN > RPC > OpenVINO > VirtGPU > WebGPU > ZenDNN > CPU_AVX2 (if model fits 50% free RAM) > IGPU_SHARED > RAM_SWAP > CPU_SCALAR**.

**Gaps/stubs in selection itself:**
- `is_hip_available/is_hexagon_available/is_zdnn_available/is_musa_available/is_opencl_available` hard-return `false` (`backend.cpp:1141-1145`) — those backends can never be auto-selected despite having implementations.
- `is_rpc_available()` always `true` (:1137-1139) so RPC would win over CPU on any machine where all GPU probes fail.
- **Bug:** selection can return `BackendType::GPU_VULKAN` (:1388-1392) but `ComputeBackend::create` has **no GPU_VULKAN case** (:961-991) → falls through to scalar CPU; the `VulkanBackend` class is only reachable via `gpu::get_vulkan_backend()`.
- Every adapter op falls back to `math::*` when `!is_initialized()` (e.g., CUDA gemm :384-386, SYCL :514, CANN :567).

## 2. Vulkan backend + iGPU zero-copy

`src/gpu_compute_vulkan.cpp` (1349 lines): full instance/device/queue setup without Vulkan headers semantics hacks — physical device picked preferring discrete GPU by parsing `VkPhysicalDeviceProperties` out of a raw byte buffer (`deviceType` read from word offset 7, :309-321), queue families parsed as raw 48-byte records (:331-346). Five **SPIR-V compute shaders embedded as uint32 arrays**: relu (:84), gelu (:108), silu (:128), add (:140), mul (:155). Pipeline path: descriptor layout with 1 storage-buffer binding + 32-byte push constants → shader module → compute pipeline, cached in an unordered_map keyed by SPIR-V bytes (:470-506); each dispatch allocates descriptor set, binds pipeline, pushes `[n]`, dispatches gx=(n+255)/256, submits with fence and waits (:508-578). Memory: `allocate()` creates DEVICE_LOCAL storage buffers returned as `VkBuffer*` handles (:758-771); upload/download via staging copies (:786-801). **Only the 5 elementwise ops run on GPU**; gemm/gemv/softmax/rms_norm/layer_norm/moe_gather/scatter/flash_attention are straight CPU fallbacks (`:986-1068`, incl. comment that GEMV previously wrongly dispatched the relu shader, :994-995). `is_initialized()` returns true even in CPU-fallback mode (:755). The `vk_*` tensor wrappers (:1145+) silently return zeros if the backend didn't init (e.g., `vk_flash_attention` :1145-1156); `vk_cross_entropy[_grad]` are pure CPU loops regardless (:1159-1200).

**Zero-copy trainer** `src/igpu_zero_copy.cpp`: same loading pattern; allocator probes for **unified memory = HOST_VISIBLE|HOST_COHERENT|DEVICE_LOCAL in one type** (`has_unified_memory()` :601-612). `allocate_unified()` requests exactly that triple, falls back to HOST_VISIBLE|HOST_COHERENT (:685-690), persistently maps and returns the host pointer (:703-715); frees tracked by mapped-pointer lookup (:718-731). `available_unified()` sums DEVICE_LOCAL heaps (:753-760). However `upload_direct/download_direct` are plain `memcpy` either way (:733-751), and `IGPUZeroCopyTrainer` computes with **scalar CPU kernels over the mapped memory**: `forward_linear` runs `cpu_zc_gemm` on unified buffers (:836-861), attention/RMSNorm/SwiGLU/cross-entropy are scalar loops (:884-955), `train_step` just computes loss (:957-960). So zero-copy allocation is real; GPU-side math is not wired up.

## 3. CUDA backend

`src/gpu_compute_cuda.cpp` (1065 lines): loads driver DLL only (:715-723), resolves ~20 symbols including `cuModuleLoadData`, `cuLaunchKernel`, async memcpy, streams, `cuMemHostRegister` (:728-749). Ships **hand-written PTX (.version 6.5, .target sm_30) as one embedded string** (:51-668) with **17 kernels**: gemm (naive 16x16-tile, one FMA per thread per k, :56-98), relu (:100), silu (:111), gelu as fast-gelu x·sigmoid(1.702x) via ex2.approx (:122), add (:133), mul (:145), scale (:157), fill (:168), softmax row kernel (:177), rmsnorm (:207), rope (:233), attention (full QK^T softmax PV per query block, non-causal, :284-405), reduce_sum/max with shared-memory tree (:407/:467), moe_gather/moe_scatter (:~510s/:566), layernorm (:605). Init: cuInit→cuDeviceGet→cuCtxCreate→two streams (compute+copy, :751-756)→`cuModuleLoadData(PTX)` (:758)→17 `cuModuleGetFunction` (:760-776). Host page-locking + async upload/download exposed in header (`include/quant/gpu_compute_cuda.h:47-54`) and implemented via `cuMemHostRegister`/stream launches (~:1000-1065). The `GPUCUDABackend` wrapper in backend.cpp only routes **gemm** through the GPU with per-call alloc/upload/compute/download/free (:383-404); every other op goes to `math::*` (:405-421) — so most CUDA kernels are implemented but not exercised through the ComputeBackend interface.

## 4. CPU SIMD kernel library

- **math.cpp** = portable scalar baseline, compiled only `#if !QUANT_AVX2` (`src/math.cpp:662`); FP16 conversions :517-553; **FP8 E4M3/E5M2 at math.cpp:564-646** — bit-exact decode (E4M3 exp==15 → NaN :564-572; E5M2 inf/NaN :574-582), round-to-nearest encode with overflow saturation (:584-622), vector converters (:624-631), and `fp8_gemm` which quantizes A/B elementwise then accumulates in f32 (:633-646).
- **math_avx2.cpp** (compiled only under QUANT_AVX2, :9-11): GEMM is a **6x16 register-blocked kernel** with `_mm256_set1_ps` broadcasts and dual `_mm256_fmadd_ps` accumulators (:19-75); AVX2 gemv/dot/axpy/squared-norm (:96-190). The FP8 family is re-implemented here (:660-668) — still scalar inner logic, but `fp8_gemm` batch-quantizes whole matrices into uint8/f32 buffers before the triple loop.
- **math_avx2_tiled.cpp** + **math_tiled.h**: cache-blocked GEMM — TILE_M=N=K=64 "tuned for L1/L2" (`math_tiled.h:13-17`); A/B panels packed to `alignas(64)` buffers (:274-288, :341-342), next-panel prefetch hint (:384-386), four transpose variants with 6x16 micro-kernels (:117-240), fused bias/ReLU/GELU/SiLU epilogues (:432-484). Header API `gemm_tiled[_bias/_act/_bias_act]` (`math_tiled.h:25-93`).
- **math_avx2_tensor.cpp**: tensor-shaped wrappers over the same AVX2 primitives (guarded QUANT_AVX2 at :9).
- **AVX512 status**: `math_avx512.cpp` compiles only `#if defined(__AVX512F__)` (:8) and provides polynomial exp approx (:22+) plus elementwise/norm/gemv only per `math_avx512.h` — **no AVX-512 GEMM kernel**. The `CPU_AVX512` backend writes its own naive `_mm512_fmadd_ps` outer-product GEMM inline in `backend.cpp:183-201` and delegates everything else to an internal CPUAVX2Backend member (:179, :203-215). CMake adds math_avx512.cpp conditionally (`CMakeLists.txt:62-63`).
- **kernel.h/kernel_production.h**: `kernel.h` = codebook/LUT GEMM family (TL1/TL2 LUT, QUANT8/QUANT4 codebook, Q3/Q6/Q12/Q24) plus `scalar/tiled/avx2[_bt]_gemm` (`include/quant/kernel.h:11-81`), implemented across `src/kernel_tl.cpp, kernel_quant8.cpp, kernel_quant4.cpp, kernel_q3/q6/q12/q24.cpp, kernel_gemm.cpp`. `kernel_production.h` = ISA-dispatched (AVX2>SSE4.1>scalar, runtime-detected) tiled/batch GEMV for the Q0/QUANT4/QUANT8 packed-weight formats (`include/quant/kernel_production.h:1-40`), impl in `src/kernel_production.cpp`.

## 5. Tests vs untestable scaffolding

**Have tests** (registered in `tests/CMakeLists.txt`; listed in suite at :209-211):
- `tests/test_math.cpp` — ReLU/SiLU/RMSNorm correctness only; **no FP8 or tiled-GEMM tests**.
- `tests/test_kernel.cpp` — scalar_gemm/tiled_gemm/avx2_gemm checked against exact 128.0 results (lines 14-45).
- `tests/test_gpu.cpp` — smoke test: probe_hardware + auto_select_backend + `is_cuda_available()` with graceful-fallback messaging (:21-31).
- `tests/test_cuda_backend.cpp` — init/alloc/free on real hardware; body skipped entirely when no NVIDIA driver (:15-27 prints "Graceful Fallback OK").

**Untested / scaffolding-only:**
- No tests at all for: Vulkan backend, igpu_zero_copy, gpu_compute_full (DX12), Metal, SYCL, CANN, HIP, OpenVINO, VirtGPU, WebGPU, ZenDNN, OpenCL, MUSA, Hexagon, zDNN, RPC, math_tiled/math_avx512 functions, kernel_production, or any FP8 path.
- Scaffolding evidence: WebGPU and VirtGPU `gemm` are plain scalar triple-loops reporting a fake fixed 1 GB of memory (`gpu_compute_webgpu.cpp:70-92`, `gpu_compute_virtgpu.cpp:50-71`); their init merely dlopens a library or opens `/dev/dri/renderD128` (`virtgpu :29-31`) without ever dispatching work. MUSA/Hexagon/zDNN/OpenCL/HIP init fails cleanly when the runtime lib is absent (`gpu_compute_musa.cpp:42`, `hexagon:41,49`, `zdnn:41,52`, `opencl:60-87`, `hip:290-306`) but are additionally locked out of auto-selection by hardcoded `false` (`backend.cpp:1141-1145`). The RPC backend always reports available (`backend.cpp:656`, :1137-1139) with per-op CPU fallback inside each call (:618-650). The zero-copy trainer's GPU claim is cosmetic: uploads are `memcpy` (`igpu_zero_copy.cpp:733-751`) and all math is scalar (:765-818).

**Net assessment:** genuinely functional paths are CPU (scalar/AVX2/tiled + codebook kernels), DX12 on Windows (runtime HLSL), CUDA gemm-on-GPU when a driver exists, and Vulkan's five elementwise shaders; everything else is well-structured probe-and-degrade scaffolding whose value is graceful fallback rather than acceleration.

# Quantization/Codec Core — Findings

## 1. Format system end-to-end

**Format IDs / BPW table.** `include/quant/types.h:22-67` defines a v3 enum (`Format : uint8_t`, rearranged 2026-08-23) with `FORMAT_COUNT = 105` (types.h:67): base10 (Q1..Q32 = 0-9), K27 (L/M/H quality tiers per integer BPW, 10-36), GRP9 (37-45), K_GRP27 (46-72), half-BPW plain9 (73-81, e.g. `Q1_5`=1.5 BPW), half GRP9 (82-90), and MXQ 4-tier mixes (91-104). `format_bpw()` (types.h:97-125) is the canonical BPW table: K variants claim *identical* BPW to their base ("same wire, different encoder search depth"), GRP claims *exact same* BPW as base, half/MXQ claim x.5.

**Wire codec.** `quantize_block_all`/`dequantize_block_all` (src/block_codec.cpp:1825, :2052) are declared "single source of truth" (include/quant/block_codec.h:11). Block size is 256 weights (`WIRE_BLOCK`, src/format_registry.cpp:17); tails are partial blocks. All bit streams are LSB-first via scalar `BitWriter`/`BitReader` (block_codec.cpp:87-118).

**Per-format payloads** (n=256 unless noted):
- Q32 raw FP32; Q24 truncated mantissa 3B/w (block_codec.cpp:1478); Q16 vmin/vmax uniform grid with FP32 header funded by packing every 4th weight at 15 bits (block_codec.cpp:1401-1447).
- Q2/Q4: Lloyd-Max level tables `kQ2Levels`/`kQ4Levels` (block_codec.cpp:14-23) + one FP16 scale funded by 16 "slot" positions that decode as 0 (`kScaleSlots`, `is_slot`, block_codec.cpp:122-133, :401).
- Q3/Q6/Q12: static Gaussian codebooks — true Lloyd-Max 8 levels (block_codec.cpp:511), symmetric N(0,1) quantile tables for 6/12 bits built once via `erfinv_approx` (:516-561); no codebook bytes travel.
- Q8: tanh-companded 256-level grid, k=0.02 (`kQ8CompandK`, block_codec.cpp:180), decode via static 256-entry LUT (:186-195).
- Q1: FP16 scale + sign bits, 16 zero slots (block_codec.cpp:1858-1873).
- Scale encoding is **one FP16 per 256-block** for lattice/fixed formats; **FP16 d per 16-weight group × 3-bit codes** for the grp16 path (block_codec.cpp:663, :713); **per-32 full FP16 scales** for the grp8/grp12 compound paths (:356-360, :879-881); **per-16 int8 scales + FP16 d** for Q6_GRP's Q6_K scheme (6.5625 BPW, 210 B/block exact, block_codec.cpp:943-954, :967); **per-group affine scale+min** (signed bias-encoded min code, a claimed improvement over GGML unsigned-min) for Q4_GRP/Q2_GRP etc. (block_codec.cpp:1163-1172, :1258).

**GRP compound scheme:** full blocks use dedicated compound layouts when they fit exactly (grp8 8.5 BPW = n bytes codes + n/32 FP16 scales, `grp8_compound_fits` block_codec.cpp:357; grp12 12.5 BPW :879); otherwise deterministic fallback to `quant_affine` or `quant_grp16`, chosen identically on encode and decode by `n`.

## 2. Encode paths / scale fitting

- **`comp8_fit_scale_fp16`** (block_codec.cpp:298-350): 3 least-squares iterations seeding from amax, then tight golden-section refine over ±25% (~14 iters, tolerance 1e-11 relative), then an FP16-ladder neighbor check (±4 codes). ~22 objective evals vs "original 70+" per its comment; objective is TRUE group MSE including clipping (`comp8_group_mse`, :248).
- **`comp8_scale_search`** (block_codec.cpp:215-245): 33-sample log-spaced coarse scan + golden section — **dead code**, no call site.
- **Golden search** appears twice more inline (grp8 fit above and `comp8_scale_search`); other paths use cheaper Lloyd loops: `grp16_fit_scale` 4 assign/refit iters + ±15% fac-grid polish (:674-711); affine variant adds closed-form 2-parameter LS (`grp16_fit_affine`, :1194-1244) and joint (d,sc)/(d,dm) refinement for 5 iterations (:1309).
- **`q1_fit_scale_fp16`** (block_codec.cpp:263-291): closed-form optimum s* = mean|w| for sign coding, then ±4 FP16-code local check.
- **Q6_K path** uses GGML-style weighted LS with weights (av+|x|) for the d-only tail and Lloyd+polish per group (:989-1069), plus the `nearest_int_q6k` magic-float rounding trick (12582912.f, :956-960).
- **MXQ quad mix** (block_codec.cpp:1520-1754): fixed percents per tier (e.g. MXQ_3.5 = 92% sign/1-bit + 1.5%/6%/0.5% tiers), magnitude-rank tier assignment via sort (:1680-1690), budget guard demotes boundary weights tier-by-tier until total bits ≤ ceil(claimed·n) (:1657-1677), GRP variants add per-32 scales on the dominant tier (:1703).

## 3. Decode paths & SIMD

**Zero SIMD in the codec** (grep for `__m256/_mm_/simd` in src/block_codec.cpp: 0 hits). Decode speed tricks: companded-Q8 LUT with a byte-aligned direct-read fast path skipping BitReader (`dequant_grp8_compound`, block_codec.cpp:382-395); LUT indexing clamped (`std::min(idx,255u)`, :492); FP24 reconstruct via memcpy (:1491-1499); magic-constant float-to-int rounding (:956). Everything else is scalar per-element bit-loops; sub-8-bit formats pay the branchy `BitReader::get` per weight. The container layer (`QUANTReader`) is mmap-based with an O(1) prebuilt block-offset index (include/quant/quant_format.h:79-102; src/quant_format.cpp:416).

## 4. FormatPlanner importance routing

- Two importance metrics: MAGNITUDE = mean(|w|·|activation|) per block (src/format_planner.cpp:26-36); FISHER_DIAG = RMS gradient × |w[start]| (:39-61).
- `allocate()` (:139-315) picks whichever of {closest single, closest twi-mix, closest four-mix} is nearest target BPW, then assigns tiers to importance-sorted blocks; last tier absorbs remainder. Single-path explicitly routes most-salient blocks to Q32 first (:293). The adaptive alternative is `FormatRegistry::allocate_mix_blocks` (src/format_registry.cpp:471-593): greedy benefit-per-byte upgrades under a HARD byte budget using a priority queue, measuring actual canonical-codec MSE per block/tier — this is the better-engineered router.
- Note: twi mixes are always empty (`build_two_mixes` returns {}, format_registry.cpp:73-76), so the planner's twi branch is effectively dead.

## 5. Dead code, hacks, inconsistencies (the important part)

**BPW contract violations / header-vs-cpp conflicts (most serious):**
- types.h says "GRP exact at same BPW (no extra)" (types.h:17-21) and `format_bpw(Q2_GRP)=2.0` etc., but the encoder writes GRP payloads at +0.5/+0.625 BPW: Q2_GRP→claimed 2.625 (block_codec.cpp:1944), Q3_GRP→3.5 (:1941), Q4_GRP→4.5 (:1938), Q6_GRP→6.5625 (:1935, 210B vs claimed 192B), Q8_GRP→8.5 (:1928), Q12_GRP→12.5, Q16_GRP→16.5, Q24_GRP→24.5. So `block_claimed_bytes` (block_codec.cpp:2257, which uses `format_bpw`) under-reports what the codec stores; registry descriptors also claim the lower BPW (format_registry.cpp:34-42), so `actual_bpw` ≠ descriptor bpw.
- Worse, **K_GRP formats silently overflow**: they call `quant_affine(..., 2.0f/3.0f/4.0f)` (block_codec.cpp:1995-1999); when the affine header doesn't fit the exact-integer budget, `affine_budget_ok` fails and falls back to `quant_grp16`, which hardcodes its own +0.5 claim regardless of the requested one (:715) — e.g. Q2_K_L_GRP emits ~78-80 B where the contract allows 65. This violates the budget contract stated at block_codec.h:14 and :65-68. The fuzz test that allegedly enforces it (tests/test_fuzz_codec.cpp:195-198) only enumerates enum values 0..37 (:171-183), so formats 38-104 — exactly the violators — are never budget-checked.
- Q6_K_L_GRP truncates its own payload to the 6.0-BPW size (`indices.resize` after `quant_6k`, block_codec.cpp:2001), chopping off scales+d so decode degrades to unscaled raw levels.

**Likely correctness bugs:**
- **Affine grid mismatch for bits==1 and bits==6**: `level_value_affine` special-cases only 2/3/8 and defaults everything else to index/15 (block_codec.cpp:1174-1179), while `nearest_level_affine` defaults to round(v·3) (:1181-1187). For Q1_5/Q_GRP_1_5 encode assumes levels {0,⅓,⅔,1} but stores only 1 bit (`bw.put(l, 1)` truncates, :2012/:2022) and decode reconstructs idx/15; Q6_5/Q_GRP_6_5 (:2016/:2026) have the same encode-{thirds}/decode-{fifteenths} mismatch without truncation. Deterministic but far worse quality than intended.
- **Codebook serialize/deserialize magic mismatch**: `CodebookQUANT8::serialize` writes 0x51554138 (src/codebook.cpp:191) but `deserialize` requires 0x4F494C34-style 0x4F494C38 (:204) → round-trip always throws. Same for QUANT4 (0x51554134 :340 vs 0x4F494C34 :353).
- **MXQ GRP dominant-tier scale count mismatch**: encoder computes `nsc = ceil(c/32)` and fits per-32 (block_codec.cpp:1703, comment ":1697 'per-32 (was per-64)'") but applies scales at `j/64` granularity (:1741) and stores ceil(c/32) FP16s; decoder computes `nsc = ceil(c/64)` (:1793) and applies j/64 (:1819) — decoder reads fewer scales than stored (trailing bytes wasted), and groups apply a scale fitted on the wrong 32-weight window.
- `score_importance_fisher` multiplies by |w[start]| — only the block's FIRST weight (format_planner.cpp:58); looks like an unintended proxy.
- Planner mix path gives the **cheapest tier to the most important blocks** (tier1_fmt=Q1 gets the top of the importance-sorted order, format_planner.cpp:199-232), contradicting the single path's salience-first intent (:293).
- `score_importance` indexes calibration activations `ad[i]` up to weights' numel with no length check (format_planner.cpp:30-33) — potential OOB read.
- `format_to_regformat` maps ALL nine half-BPW GRP formats to `RegFormat::Q1` (format_registry.h:177) — clearly wrong lossy mapping.

**Dead code / stale artifacts:**
- Unreachable statements after returns: block_codec.cpp:1986, :2010, :2184, :2210 (leftover Q32-copy lines from an earlier layout).
- Unused statics in anonymous namespace: `comp8_scale_search` (:215), `f32_bits`/`f32_from_bits` (:158/:164), `level_value_r`/`nearest_level_r` (:407/:412).
- Truncated/mangled comments: orphan fragment "// = n*bits exactly…" (block_codec.cpp:397); lattice comment cut mid-expression and fused with the Q6_GRP comment (:936-943); block_codec.h:47 begins with dangling "FP16 d." and its whole layout table (:18-31) still documents retired v2 names (QUANT32, QUANT_Q0, TWI_MIX) absent from the v3 enum.
- `constants.h:44 QUANT_FORMAT_COUNT = 15` vs types.h:67 `FORMAT_COUNT = 105`; `QUANT_Q0_CENTROIDS`/`QUANT_Q0_BPW` (constants.h:42,:48) reference a removed format.
- `src/adapters/tools/quant_import.cpp` references nonexistent enum values (`Format::QUANT1`, `QUANT_Q0`, `QUANT_Q1_GRP`, ~line 48) — stale against current types.h; won't reflect the real enum.
- Fuzz test skips slot 19 believing it "a deliberate gap" (test_fuzz_codec.cpp:171-183), but in the v3 enum 19 = `Q4_K_L`, a valid format; its kCaps table also has duplicate MXQ rows (:137-150).
- `parse_format_name` only knows the 19 singles (format_registry.cpp:446-452) — names like "Q2_K_M"/"Q8_5" return an empty descriptor with id 0.
- Minor: `select_best_mix` has an unused shadowed `best_mse` (format_registry.cpp:343-344); `Codebook::quantize` mutates mutable EMA accumulators inside a const query (codebook.h:51-54, codebook.cpp:177-178); trainable `CodebookQ3/Q6/Q12` classes are unused by the wire codec (which uses static tables) and belong to the parallel legacy pipeline in `engines/quant/codec.{h,cpp}`; `int8_quant.cpp` is a clean standalone absmax scaler with no issues found; `quant_import.h` does not exist anywhere (the importer is the CLI tool above).

**Net assessment:** encode-side fitting is genuinely sophisticated (true-MSE golden search, joint d/sc refinement, signed affine mins), decode is deliberately scalar/LUT-based, but the "honest BPW everywhere" promise advertised in block_codec.h is not honored by the GRP/K_GRP families, and the affine 1-/6-bit grids plus the codebook magic constants are outright bugs.

----- [ASSISTANT] 2026-08-24T14:40 -----

[thinking] Very informative. Key observations for RL:
- PPOTrainer::train_step computes losses in scalar floats — no autograd backward at all. It manually sets critic grads (and even those are mathematically wrong: uses states as if they were the hidden input to the critic, indexing `states.data<float>()[i * states.dim(1) + j]` — treating first 64 elements of state as hidden). Policy loss/entropy/kl computed but never backpropagated to policy parameters. So PPO is essentially a metric-computation stub; policy weights never updated by PPOTrainer itself.
- DPOTrainer::train_step computes loss as float, then just calls optimizer_->step() with whatever grads exist (none) — fake update path.
- GRPO: real graph-based per-sample advantage-weighted CE. Good.
- RLVR: CE loss scaled by -reward on gradients; he

[thinking] Confirmed the dummy {1,1} aux tensor issue at moe_trainer.cpp:285-288 — `compute_aux_loss(dummy_logits, dummy_indices)` with {1,1} tensors. Note that compute_aux_loss returns 0 if numel==0 but here numel is 1, so it computes a garbage aux loss from a single dummy token: n_tokens=1, n_experts=1 → aux = 1*sum(f*p) = 1*(1*1) = 1.0 constant! Actually f_0 = probs_per_expert[0]/1 = e^{row[0]-row[0]}=1; p_0=1; aux_loss = n_experts(1)*1 = 1. So aux_loss is constant 1.0 * coef added to loss value only for metrics; and importantly the total_loss_val written into loss.data() includes coef*aux (constant), which does scale the gradient of CE slightly (since they overwrite the loss value used for backward). Also load_balance/zloss are read from l.load_balance_loss / l.z_loss floats set during forward —

[thinking] So Linear::forward uses AutogradEngine::matmul_op → gradients DO flow into expert weights and router weight through the matmul nodes... but the moe_dispatch_batched output is a fresh tensor assembled by raw pointer writes. The autograd chain: loss -> (CE node) -> logits -> ... The `output` of moe_dispatch_batched is a brand-new Tensor with no registered node output. So when MoEBlock does AutogradEngine::add_op(ffn_output, attn_out) — add_op records a node with inputs {ffn_output, attn_out} — during backward, gradient flows to ffn_output's data pointer, accum in accum map for that pointer, then stack.push_back(ffn_output.data()) — output_to_node_.find(ffn_output.data()) fails → pop, dead end. So NO gradient reaches experts/router via backprop through the MoE output!

[thinking] ZeroOptimizer uses a `thread_local` BarrierState in zero_barrier — this is a fundamental bug for multi-THREAD simulation: each thread has its OWN barrier state, so a barrier can never synchronize across different threads (each thread's counter increments independently; with one thread per rank... wait, if each rank is a separate thread, tls_barrier is per-thread, count never reaches world_size → deadlock). Actually if all "ranks" run on the same thread sequentially it would work (count resets when reaching world_size — a single thread calling it world_size times passes). But as written with thread_local state, cross-thread sync impossible. Also comm_buffer_ accumulation + barrier pattern: single-thread sequential simulation works only if the same thread calls reduce_scatter per-rank loops.

# InNova Training Stack — Findings

## 1. Autograd engine
- **Design**: singleton `AutogradEngine` (`include/quant/autograd.h:57`, `src/autograd_engine.cpp`). Ops are recorded as `AutogradNode{fn, inputs, outputs}` into a flat vector; outputs are keyed by raw `data()` pointer in `output_to_node_` (weak_ptr). `backward()` (`autograd_engine.cpp:352`) is a manual consumer-count/topological-stack walk (not a queue-based tape); seeds loss with ones if no grad. 12 op types with hand-written backward kernels: matmul, add, silu, mul, RMSNorm, CE, RoPE, SDPA (with GQA), bias-add, embedding, flatten, transpose + gradient checkpointing (`CheckpointFn`, `autograd.h:25`; recompute in backward at `autograd_engine.cpp:391`).
- **Thread safety**: mutex-guarded node registry and param registry; `backward()` holds the engine mutex for the entire pass (so backward is globally serialized — safe but single-threaded). Param registry is self-cleaning via `~Tensor` → `unregister_parameter` plus a static `registry_alive_` teardown flag (`autograd_engine.cpp:13-33`), and stale-entry guards with global hit counters `g_guard_*` (`autograd_engine.cpp:348, 412-417`).
- **STE**: real and used two ways. (a) Graph STE: `qat::FakeQuantizeFunction::backward` returns identity grad; `LSQFunction::backward` implements clip-mask STE + per-element scale grad (`src/qat.cpp:14-110`). (b) The dense `Trainer` QAT path instead mutates weights in place (backup → fake-quant memcpy → restore after backward, `src/trainer_core.cpp:266-306, 408-412`) — a weight-mutation STE, not a graph node. `STEQuantizer` (`src/ste_quantizer.cpp`) does quantize→dequantize forward only.
- **Correctness testing**: `tests/test_gradient_check.cpp` checks one analytic-vs-numerical gradient (rel err < 1e-2) and SGD finiteness. Coverage is thin relative to 12 ops.

## 2. Trainer architecture
- `Trainer` (`include/quant/trainer.h:240`, impl split across `trainer_core.cpp` / `trainer_data.cpp` / `trainer_rl*.cpp`): compile() registers DenseModel params into autograd + optimizer; default optimizer is **Adafactor** (`trainer_core.cpp:119-123`).
- Loop: `fit()` → per batch: zero_grad → `gradient_accumulation_steps` × `micro_step` on the **same batch** (`trainer_core.cpp:189-191`) → optional unscale → clip → noise inject → step → EMA. Features: label smoothing (returns per-token tensor but is fed to CE op), R-Drop, curriculum, data aug, dynamic loss scaling, EMA, checkpoint save/load of Adam moments (`trainer_core.cpp:511-576`).
- **Optimizers** (`include/quant/optimizer.h`, `src/optimizer.cpp`): 13 total — AdamW (schedules incl. warmup-cosine/restart), Adam, SGD+Nesterov, Adamax, NAdam, RAdam, Lion, RMSProp, Ranger(RAdam+LookAhead), Adabound, Lamb, LARS, NovoGrad. **Adafactor is genuinely factorized** (row/col EMAs of squared grads, r*c/r̄ second moment, update clipping, zero-moment NaN guard) at `optimizer.cpp:338-398`.
- **Loss-scale bug (dense + MoE)**: micro_step multiplies the stored loss *value* by loss_scale (`trainer_core.cpp:314-317`), but `backward()` always seeds grads with 1.0 (`autograd_engine.cpp:353-357`), so the scale never reaches gradients while `unscale_gradients` still divides by it (`trainer_core.cpp:153-162`) — enabling mixed_precision silently shrinks effective updates by ~128x. Same pattern in `moe_trainer.cpp:300-302`. "Mixed precision" is fp32 compute + fake loss-scale bookkeeping.
- **MTP**: heads exist (`model.h:55`, built in `src/model.cpp:25-27`) and `Trainer::mtp_loss` is implemented (`trainer_core.cpp:742-769`), but **nothing ever calls mtp_loss or forward_mtp in any training loop**; `TrainConfig::mtp_loss_weight` (`trainer.h:68`) is unused. Dead wiring.

## 3. RL stack reality check
- **GRPO — REAL and tested**: per-sample advantage-weighted CE inside the graph, `(1/G)·Σ adv_i·CE_i` (`src/trainer_rl.cpp:606-628`), group-relative normalization (`:581-589`); collects Dense+MoE params (`collect_grpo_params`, `:534-575`). `beta` is only grad damping, explicitly not KL (`:632-641`). Tests: `test_grpo.cpp` (advantage-separation behavior, zero-variance, MoE), `test_rll_e2e.cpp` (finite-loss GRPO on HybridMoE).
- **PPO — metric shell, not a trainer**: clipped surrogate/GAE/KL/entropy are all computed as scalar floats (`trainer_rl.cpp:160-243`) but there is **no backward pass and no policy update**; the only "gradients" are hand-written critic-head grads that index `states` as if they were hidden activations (`trainer_rl.cpp:269-297` — mathematically wrong). Untested.
- **DPO — fake update path**: Bradley-Terry-style sigmoid loss computed correctly as floats (`trainer_rl.cpp:342-396`) but then just calls `optimizer_->step()` with whatever grads exist (none — forwards happen outside a graph); in `RLHFPipeline::dpo_finetune` no optimizer is even attached (`trainer_rl_ops.cpp:379`). No tests.
- **Reward model — REAL**: 3-layer MLP with manual but complete backprop of pairwise logistic (Bradley-Terry) loss, accuracy logging, save/load (`src/reward.cpp:132-345`). Tested indirectly? No dedicated test file found.
- **RLHF pipeline — partially fake**: `generate_comparisons` sets `chosen_ids == rejected_ids` and fabricates rewards differing by a constant −0.01 (`trainer_rl.cpp:486-494`), so preference data is synthetic. RLVR = CE scaled by −reward (`trainer_rl.cpp:750-758`), verify() is substring match (`:807`).

## 4. MoE training
- Routing: real top-k softmax router (`softmax_with_topk`, `src/moe_variants.cpp:97`), batched dispatch (`moe_dispatch_batched`, `:767`); variants Sparse/Soft/Hierarchical/MoMoE/ExpertChoice/Hash/CrossLayer/MultiModal/MMoE/DeepSeek in `moe_variants.cpp`; second router impl in `mommoe_block.cpp` (TopKRouter + cross-modal attention).
- **Critical: routed experts & routers get ZERO gradient.** `moe_dispatch_batched` reassembles output via raw pointer writes into a fresh Tensor (`moe_variants.cpp:772, 810-821`), which breaks the autograd chain — during backward the FFN branch dead-ends (no node registered for that buffer; `autograd_engine.cpp:387-389`). Router logits are consumed only as floats for load-balance stats. So MoETrainer effectively trains only attention/embeddings/lm_head/**shared expert**; `test_moe_training.cpp` asserts only finiteness so this passes unnoticed.
- Aux load-balance loss: computed correctly from router stats (`compute_load_balance_loss`, `moe_variants.cpp:140`) but as a **float scalar added post-hoc to the loss value** (`moe_trainer.cpp:278-301`) — no gradient path, it only inflates reported metrics. Confirmed known issue: dummy `{1,1}` tensors at `moe_trainer.cpp:285-287` make `aux_loss ≡ 1.0` constant.
- `MoETrainer::compute_expert_utilization` double-counts (`total_util += layer_util` where layer_util derives from already-accumulated total, `moe_trainer.cpp:429-447`).
- **Expert prefetcher**: `ExpertPrefetcher` itself is fully implemented (pinned buffers, prefetch thread, LRU, CUDA async upload — `src/expert_prefetch.cpp:89-356`, tested in `test_expert_prefetch.cpp`). But `MoEModel::init_prefetcher` and `wire_prefetcher_sources` declared at `moe_model.h:65,70` have **no implementation anywhere** (repo-wide grep finds only the header declaration) and `prefetcher` is never constructed — so the model never uses it.

## 5. Distributed / FSDP / ZeRO / DDP
- **No NCCL/MPI/gloo anywhere** (grep confirms). All collectives are shared-memory/thread based ("simulates multi-GPU", `distributed.h:18`): `DistributedContext` all_reduce/broadcast/all_gather via mutex+barrier (`src/distributed.cpp:22-115`), plus a `RingAllReduce`, `ParameterServer` (async PS with staleness handling) — all in-process constructs.
- `expert_parallel.cpp` additionally has a **real TCP backend** with ring allreduce over sockets, capacity-aware routing, fault-tolerant node manager (`src/expert_parallel.cpp:1180-1250+`), but the `ExpertParallel` actually used by MoETrainer (`moe_model.cpp:506-700`) is the single-process simulation: with `num_ranks>1` in one process, `ctx_->all_reduce` → `barrier()` waits for arrivals that never come → **deadlock**. `sync_gradients` divides expert grads by ranks (`moe_trainer.cpp:231-242`). Only the partition logic is tested (`test_expert_parallel.cpp`).
- FSDP (`src/fsdp.cpp`): genuine ZeRO 1/2/3 sharding, stage-3 allgather-installs-full-params before forward/backward, real autograd backward seeded with a documented pseudo-loss (CE vs class 0 scaled by loss_value, `fsdp.cpp:236-260`), Adafactor over 1×N shard views. Untested (zero references in tests/tools/bench).
- `ZeroOptimizer` (`src/zero_optimizer_core.cpp`): ZeRO-3 param sharding with owner = idx % world_size; its `zero_barrier` uses **thread_local state** (`:166-180`) so it cannot synchronize across threads — another latent deadlock if ever run multi-threaded. Also untested.
- DDP: bucketed grad sync + async thread mode (`src/ddp.cpp`), untested.

## 6. Continual learning
- Real and fairly complete: `CompressedReplayBuffer` (16-level codebook, 4-bit packed entries, Fisher-weighted insertion eviction/importance resampling, `src/continual_engine.cpp:20-140s`); `ECCState` Fisher diagonal + anchors + regularizer; `ForgettingBenchmark`; wired into the dense Trainer: replay double-backward with delta-scaling by replay_ratio (`trainer_core.cpp:356-387`), EWC gradient injection `g += 2λF(θ−anchor)` (`:389-406`), Fisher/replay updates each step (`:414-446`), task-boundary anchoring (`:805-828`). Anti-collapse: orthogonal projection, ExperienceReplayBuffer, DistributionWatchdog w/ rollback, entropy floor — all implemented (`continual_engine.cpp:414-543`), tested by `test_continual_anticollapse.cpp`. Note duplicate decl of `apply_orthogonal_projection` (`continual_engine.h:261,264`).

## 7. Dead code / fake-ish / untested-but-claimed
- **Dead**: `Trainer::mtp_loss` + `DenseModel::mtp_loss` + `mtp_heads` never invoked from training (`trainer_core.cpp:742`, `model.cpp:416-422`); `TrainConfig::mtp_loss_weight` unused. R-Drop: `fit()` adds `rdrop_loss` *after* backward/clear with engine disabled (`trainer_core.cpp:193-195, 604`) — affects only the reported loss, never gradients. `AsymmetricReward` class defined but unused (`reward.cpp:359`). `MoETrainer::accumulate_expert_stats` never called. `CheckpointFn` exists and works but nothing in the model forward sets checkpoints except tests.
- **Unimplemented**: `MoEModel::init_prefetcher` / `wire_prefetcher_sources` (`moe_model.h:65-70`) — header-only, confirmed by grep.
- **Fake-ish**: RLHF comparison generation (identical chosen/rejected ids + constant reward offset, `trainer_rl.cpp:486-491`); PPO policy "training" without backprop; DPO optimizer step without gradients; mixed-precision loss scaling (value-only, §2); MoE aux/z/load-balance losses as non-differentiable scalars; `ExpertParallel::alltoall_experts` computes an all_gather then discards it (`moe_model.cpp:545-550`); MoETrainer `mp_quantize_forward/mp_restore_master` are memcpy/EMA stubs, no actual quantization (`moe_trainer.cpp:564-583`).
- **Untested but present**: entire distributed suite (FSDP/ZeRO/DDP/RingAllReduce/ParameterServer — no test references), PPOTrainer/DPOTrainer/RewardModel/RLHFPipeline, `NativeQUANTTrainer` (real two-timescale loop over native QUANT store, `native_trainer.cpp`, tested by test_native_quant*), MMAP DataLoader & StreamingDataset (implemented, `trainer_data.cpp:135-165`, Windows+POSIX mmap).

**Bottom line**: dense SFT training (autograd + Adafactor family + EWC/replay continual), GRPO, reward model, LoRA-style RankAdapterEngine/KnowledgeExpansion (`fine_tuning.cpp`, graph-connected deltas, merge layout-aware at `:687-708`, tested in `test_fine_tuning.cpp`) are real. MoE training trains everything except the experts/routers themselves (broken graph through the dispatcher); PPO/DPO are accounting shells; distributed/FSDP/ZeRO/DDP are single-node simulations with deadlock hazards and zero test coverage; NCCL does not exist in the tree.

# InNova Project Exploration Report

## 1. tools/ — CLI tools (targets defined in `CMakeLists.txt:336-388`, `gle_report` at :494)

| Executable | Source | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `quant_infer` | infer.cpp | Generate text from `.quant` model: `quant_infer <model.quant> [prompt] [--model-dir DIR]` (infer.cpp:12) |
| `quant_train` | train.cpp | Generic trainer: `--model model.quant --data data.txt` (train.cpp:63); links quant_dense |
| `train_64m` | train_64m.cpp | 64M-param dense pretraining harness; returns TrainResult{train/val loss, tokens, elapsed} via NativeTrainer |
| `quant_finetune` | finetune.cpp | `--model base.quant --data finetune.txt --output finetuned.quant` (finetune.cpp:43) |
| `quant_convert` | convert.cpp (20.3K) | rawfp32→`.quant` converter using format_planner/codebook/block_codec; `--target-bpw` option |
| `k3_convert` | k3_convert.cpp | K3 MXFP4 HF checkpoint→`.quant` converter skeleton (G-4); dry-run validates layout + MXFP4 S1E2M1 dequant probe (file header, :1-4) |
| `quant_evaluate` | evaluate.cpp | Eval runner: `--task all`, batch/context/max-tokens; uses eval.h + qwen35 tokenizer |
| `quant_info` | info.cpp | Dump `.quant` header: magic/version/flags/config size + tensor names via QUANTReader |
| `quant_bench` | bench.cpp | Kernel micro-bench + optional model inference bench (kernel/size/prompt args) |
| `bench_speed` | bench_speed.cpp | Training speed: dense model hidden=512, 14 layers, B8×S2048 forward+backward timing; built under benches (CMakeLists:461) |
| `quant_serve` | serve.cpp | HTTP serving via quant_production lib; port 8080, workers/batch/max_tokens |
| `quant_server` | quant_server.cpp (617 lines) | Standalone socket HTTP API (port 9090, temp/top-k/top-p). L017 hardening: `kClientTimeoutMs=30000` (:489), request-line cap 8192B→HTTP 414 (:490,:521), 64KB body buffer overflow→HTTP 413 (:503,:509-511) |
| `quant_quantize` | quantize.cpp | PTQ to any Format (Q1–Q32 + GRP variants parsed at :15-25) |
| `quant_format_list` | format_list.cpp | Prints `FormatRegistry::get_format_table()` (whole file is 10 lines) |
| `generate_comparison_visuals` | generate_comparison_visuals.cpp | Reads bench_format_comparison.csv → generates docs/COMPARISON_CHARTS.md with SVGs; "no hardcoded numbers" (header) |
| `gle_report` | gle_report.cpp | Reads `.research/telemetry/events.jsonl`→markdown GLE summary; `--init` writes hash-chained genesis RUN_STARTED event |

Ops scripts: `deploy_wdac.ps1` (install WDAC policy XML into CiPolicies\Active to trust signed binaries), `disable_smartscreen.ps1` (registry toggle), `sign.ps1` (self-signed cert + sign all exes), `sign_adapter.ps1` (CN=ORIGIN LABS adapter-edition signing), `run_tests.ps1`/`run_all_tests.ps1` (run all `test_*.exe` in build\Release, tally PASS/FAIL). `tools/wdac/` holds policy xml/bin artifacts.

## 2. engines/

- **inference/** — `InferenceEngine`: load `.quant`, `generate`/`generate_with_stats`/`generate_stream`; stats incl. tok/s, peak memory, KV-cache utilization (engines/inference/inference.h:20-46); stream.cpp for streaming.
- **quant/** — `quant8` namespace: `QUANT8Encoder/Decoder`, CodecConfig block_size=256, codebook_size=256, train_iters=20 (engines/quant/codec.h:11-21) — k-means codebook encoding to indices+codebook.
- **trainer/dense/** — DenseTrainer + DataLoader + checkpoint save/load; TrainConfig includes QAT flags (use_qat, qat_bits, LSQ) (engines/trainer/dense/trainer.h:13-30).
- **trainer/moe/** — Multi-modal MoE: `ModalityType` enum (TEXT…META_COGNITION) and MoEConfig allocating experts per modality (moe.h:10-52); subdirs vision/image/audio/ocr/text/video/embeddings each own cpp/h.
- **trainer/multimodal/** — Same modality split, separate library.
- **Relationship to src/**: engines/ are high-level application facades composed from src/ primitives, packaged as libs `quant_engine` (CMakeLists:169-177), `quant_dense` (:181-187), `quant_moe` (:189-209), `quant_multimodal` (:283-306). src/ owns core tensor/math/format/kernel/model/trainer layers they call into.

## 3. tests/ — 56 tests (tests/CMakeLists.txt:27-227)

Labels/categories: **core** (~25: tensor, math, format, kernel, model, kda/mla attention, hybrid scheduler/model/moe/expert, both tokenizers, mix components L013/L014, sha256_corrupt, format_registry_complete, grp/fp32-gather/bpw150 proofs, block_codec, quant_mix, protected, debug), **training** (16: trainer, training [600s timeout, :78], gradient_check, mixed_precision_proof, quant_engines, quant_convergence, native_quant(+_moe), moe_training, grpo/rll_e2e/rollout_worker RL, training_features, fine_tuning, native_weight, expert_parallel), **inference** (inference_opt, inference_batch, ops, paged_kv_4m, agi, agi_flywheel, multimodal(+encoders), production), **gpu** (test_gpu WIN32-only :130-141, cuda_backend), **distributed** (moe_crash_repro, expert_prefetch, test_all), **gle** (gle_telemetry), **codec** (fuzz_codec), **continual** (anticollapse). Timeouts 60s default / 300s long; COST-based parallel scheduling (:202-224).

**Known-failing** (documented `.research/workbench.md:57` and `.research/claim_ledger.md` C-07: last full run 2 FAILED, stale vs suite of 56):
- `test_quant_mix.cpp` (34.4K, largest test): asserts TWI_MIX@1.5 (2-tier Q1/Q4) and QUAD_MIX `MXQ_3_5_GRP`@3.5 BPW (4-tier Q1/Q3/Q8/Q32) registry claims (:106-113); BPW hard cap `bytes ≤ ceil(bpw·n/8)` (+1B/tail block) over 14 tensor sizes (:124-163); quality ladder beating Q2/Q1_GRP@2.0 (:203-209); priority-wise budget spending (:273-303); row-aligned narrow tensors (:309-317); PTQ round-trip + fine-tune re-quant. Failure mode: **QUAD_MIX@3.5_GRP byte-budget violation 128>120** (workbench blocker, mapped to Phase 3 L036).
- `test_fuzz_codec.cpp`: fixed-seed `mt19937_64(0xCAFEF00DBEEF4211)`, 10,000 blocks × 37 formats = 370k roundtrips over uniform/gaussian(σ∈{0.05,0.1,1.0})/sparse/outlier distributions; asserts payload budget contract, finite decode, Q32 bit-exactness, per-family NMSE caps (:1-22). Failure: **TWI_MIX quality bound**.

## 4. bench/

- **bench_format_comparison.cpp** (689 lines) — central honest head-to-head: all Q-series formats through the real production codec + baselines (FP16, INT8, GGUF Q8_0/Q6_K, BitNet b1.58, binary) implemented in-file on identical weights; datasets = deterministic gaussian **and** real weights from an actual short training run (mini-train warmup_steps=5, :297); constants kN=65536, `kWarmupReps=3, kTimedReps=7`, median+stddev (:26-44); emits CSV (root `bench_format_comparison.csv`) + ASCII PSNR chart + verdicts (:600-631). Has stale `bench_format_comparison.cpp.bak`.
- **bench_poc_benchmark.cpp** (38.9K) — PoC restricted to in-house QUANT/GRP formats + QUANT_MIX importance routing (FormatPlanner) + native STE training; explicitly no external schemes (header :2-6).
- **bench_gpt2_inference.cpp** (24.5K) — real GPT-2 weight quantization quality: cosine similarity, dequant error, timing.
- Others: `bench_kernels` (GEMM/kernel micro), `bench_inference`, `bench_quality`, `bench_all` ("J1-J15" sweep incl. MoE/backend/autograd), `bench_hardware` (hardware probe built *from tests/test_bench.cpp*, CMakeLists:448), `bench_quant_quant` (quantized×quantized GEMM), `bench_training`, `bench_multimodal`.

## 5. sops/

**SOPS = Sextillion Operations Per Second**, a replacement compute metric for FLOPS on quantized workloads: counts information-weighted ops (each element weighted by info density vs FP32). Key claims in `docs/SOPS_SPEC.md` v1.0 (36K, TOC at :9-30): Q4=8×, Q0(1.5-bit)=21.33×, Q1-bit=32× SOPS advantage; measured 0.092 pSOPS on 12-core AVX2; gap to 1 ZSOPS ≈ 10.9 billion×. Code: `sops/sops_scheduler.{h,cpp}` — singleton priority-queue task scheduler (Priority LOW→CRITICAL, estimated_ops, atomic op/task counters, `throughput_sops()`); built as `quant_sops` lib with `src/sops_integration.cpp` (CMakeLists:471-477); consumed by quant_continual and quant_speculative. Executables: `bench_sops` (Type-1 FMA stress + Type-2 GEMV throughput), `sops_info` (format table with bpw + info-weight), `sops_train_sim` (training simulation on quantized weights).

## 6. docs/RESEARCH/*.md — one-line takeaways

- **01-bitnet-b1.58-analysis.md** — BitNet b1.58 (ternary {-1,0,1}, 1.58bpw, arXiv 2402.17764) is the key prior art; multiplication-free add/sub compute.
- **02-bitnetcpp-lossless-analysis.md** — bitnet.cpp's "lossless" claim only means lossless vs already-ternary weights, not vs FP32.
- **04-vqvae-residual-coding.md** — Residual VQ reduces but can never eliminate distortion; 0% loss via RVQ is off the table.
- **05-rate-distortion-bounds.md** — Shannon R(D) gaussian-source bounds give the theoretical minimum bits/weight floors for the format ladder.
- **06-ste-training-in-format.md** — STE (identity gradient through round/clip) makes training natively inside the quantized format feasible.
- **07-dynamic-routing-gamps.md** — Literature (DynaQuant AAAI'26, DMR ICML'26 with GRPO) validates dynamic per-layer/per-input precision routing behind QUANT_MIX.
- **08-final-verdict.md** — Mathematical 0% loss is impossible (rate-distortion), practical output-equivalence too; the honest claim is bounded distortion.
- **09-quant8-256-centroids.md** — Analyzes whether 256-centroid k-means blocks (1 B/weight + 1024 B codebook) reach FP32 quality.
- Extra notes: RESEARCH.md (index), actionable_improvements.md, agi_papers.md, distributed_research.md, quant_idx_research.md.

## 7. publication/

- **whitepaper/** — main.tex includes 13 chapters (main.tex:284-296): 01 introduction, 02 oil_formats, 03 mixed_precision, 04 sops, 05 native_training, 06 continual_learning, 07 mtp_speculative, 08 thread_safety, 09 empirical_results, 10 future_directions, 11 gpu_backend, 12 distributed, 13 conclusion; figures/ + references.bib; compiled main.pdf committed.
- **arxiv/paper.tex** (311 lines) — "MYTHOS: Native Quantized Intelligence": §1 Introduction; §2 OIL Format System (Quantization Barrier, Trimix Policy); §3 SOPS compute metric; §4 Native Training Without FP32 Master Weights (STE, PAC-Bayes bounds, exponential convergence, stability, diagonal dominance — theorem/corollary environments); §5 Empirical Results (multi-scale bench, GPT-2 analysis, storage at scale, industrial comparison); §6 Conclusion. Plus references.bib.
- **CLAIMS_LEDGER.md** at publication root (already known).
